Եւ Յակոբ ասէ. լուծան ջիղք ձեռին բազկաց նոցա Ճեռամբ Գղաւրին Յակովբայ» Որք փնքեանս եւ ոչ աստուած դոան առնեին, եւ իւրեանց զաւրութիւն պարծեին՝ լուծան, եւ եղեն իբըեւ զոչինչ, ի բանեն լաղթագարեալք, եւ ազգս ի ցրուժն տուեալք ։ Եւ Յակոբ ասէ. լուծան ջիղք ձեռին բազկաց նոցա Ճեռամբ Գղաւրին Յակովբայ» Որք փնքեանս եւ ոչ աստուած դոան առնեին, եւ իւրեանց զաւրութիւն պարծեին՝ լուծան, եւ եղեն իբըեւ զոչինչ, ի բանեն լաղթագարեալք, եւ ազգս ի ցրուժն տուեալք ։ « Ἐκεῖθεν ὁ κατισχύσας Ἰσραὴλ παρὰ θεοῦ πατρός σου >, καὶ ἐδοήθησέν σοι ὁ θεὸς ὁ ἐμός », τίς ἄρα ἐστὶν ὁ κατισχύσας τὸν Ἰσραὴλ καὶ βοηθήσας τῷ ἑαυτοῦ παιδὶ ἢ μόνος θεός; ὡς καὶ διὰ ᾿ἩΗσαΐου « Ἰακὼδ ὁ παῖς μου, ἀντιλήψομαι αὐτοῦ" Ἰσραὴλ ὁ ἐκλεκτός μου, προσεδέξατο αὐτὸν ἣ ψυχή μου ». καὶ πάλιν λέγει" « μὴ φοδοῦ ὅτι κατῃσχύνθης, μηδὲ ἐντραπῇς, ὅτι ὠνειδίσθης »" διὰ τοῦτο ἀντὶ τῶν ὕδρεων < ἐπ’ αὐτὸν γενομένων > ἀνταποδιδοὺς αὐτῷ τὰς εὐλογίας ϑοῦ ϑορῶ οδο οδῶσθαρο ϑορῶ ϑοϑοῦυ ϑοδοῦ». ®> ϑοδοθοὺ ὅ96 οδο Θωϑοςρϑοῦ οδο δοδοϑορορῶν @> ϑορθοὺ ῶϑοδο ϑοὺ 3G φϑοῦονο σῶν, βϑοοοῶρο δοῶοογ» οὐδοῦ. @> βοϑϑοδ @> ϑορῶϑο οὐδ. δοϑοδοῦ. δυδϑᾳνορῶφοο. > δηθὺ ὁϑοῦ > ὕω» ϑοβργοῦν φῦ Անտի Է` որ վաւրացաւ Իսրայել լաստուծոլ Հաւր քո, եւ աւդնեաց քեզ աս- տուածն իճ ε ճ Էր՝ որ զաւրացոյցն ղիսրալեղ եւ աւղնեաց իւրոչ ճանկանն` եթե ոչ միալն աստուած. որպես եւ Եսալեաւ ասէ. "From there comes He who strengthens Israel: from the God of <your> (277) father. And the God who is my God has come to your aid. Who then is he who has strengthened Israel and has come to the aid of his own child, if not God alone? As also well, through Isaiah, he (God) says: 'Jacob, my child (278), I will support him; Israel, my chosen one, my soul has delighted in him.' And again he says: 'Do not fear, for you have been confounded (279); nor have shame, for you have been outraged.' This being so, in compensation for the violences <of which he was the object>, [giving] him in return the blessings (280), he says:

"By virtue of the blessing of the breasts, and of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother (285)". "Of the blessing of the breasts". Either of the two Testaments, from which came the preaching announcing in advance the future appearance of the Word in the world; breasts thanks to which (286) both He has milk for us, and He feeds (us) (287), presenting us to God in the capacity of children. Or else, it is the breasts of Mary, (breasts) that he suckled, that he designates there, (breasts) which were blessed, to which - and in an acclamation - a woman said: "Blessed is the womb that bore you! and the breasts that you have suckled."

Ὁῶοῦᾳ5ν» T b θϑοδοουψοῶ οῤιηοδυδὺ
By adding and saying: "And of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother" the prophet proclaims in advance a spiritual mystery. He could, in effect, say: "And of the blessing of the womb of your mother", in order, by this expression, to indicate Mary, by the womb of whom the Word was nine months carried. Well! it is not that which he said, but rather he says: "And of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother (288)." Of these two things, by having thus joined them together, he made of them but one, so that it might be well understood that to this
«οὐ ϑωῤδδοδοῦοο δοϑωσρβοῦωδ ϑοθουῦο. δῶρ Ն. դլարել նորա եւ ասել՝ եե արդանդ աւրգնութեան գաւր քո եւ մաւր քո, խորճուրդ Հոգեւոր լառաջագոլն երեւեցուցանէ ճարդարեն, քանզի կարեր ասել. եւ արդանդ աւրգնուխեան մաւր քո, ղի ճառիւն նշանակեսցե ἡ Մարիամ, որով արգանդիւ չլայաւ բանն ։ Ալ ոչ ալսպես ասաց, ալլ ասե. արգանդ աւրգնուքնան Հաւր քո եւ καὶ μητρός σου », τὰ ἀμφότερα συνάψας ἕν ἐποίησεν, ἵνα νοηθῇ αὐτοῦ < καὶ τὸ κατὰ πνεῦμα χαὶ τὸ > κατὰ σάρχα᾽ ἦν γὰρ ὁ λόγος ἐκ καρδίας πατρὸς καὶ ἐκ σπλάγχνων ἁγίων « ὡς > ἐκ μήτρας πατρὸς γενόμενος. ὡς καὶ διὰ τοῦ προφήτου λέγει᾽ «ἐξηρεύξατο ἣ καρδία μου λόγον ἀγαθόν ». ἦν δὲ τὸ κατὰ σάρκα ἐπ’ ἐσχάτων ἐκ παρθενικῆς μήτρας κυοφορούμενος, ἵνα καὶ ἐκ μήτρας μητρὸς πάλιν γενόμενος < φάνῃ > φανερός. < ὡς > καὶ διὰ < τοῦ προφήτου >
person belongs <both that which is according to the spirit and that which is> according to the flesh (289). For the Word drew his origin from a heart of a father and from holy [entrails], born <as he was> (290) from a womb of a father (291). As also well, through the prophet, he (the Father) says: "My heart has uttered a good Word." On the other hand, according to the flesh, in the last times, he drew his origin from a virginal womb after having been carried there nine months, so that, after being born a second time, from a womb of a mother also, he might appear visible (292). <As also well, through the prophet, (he himself) says: "Thus says the Lord> (293) who formed me from the womb (of my mother) to be his servant." And, through Jeremiah, (the Father) says: "Before forming you in the womb (of your mother), I knew you; and before you had come out of her womb, I sanctified you." Since then the Word was begotten both according to the spirit and according to the flesh, as being in fact both God and man, it is with reason that the prophet (Jacob) used the name "womb" for a father and for a mother; which (294), if it were not thus understood, could appear to some
λέγει: «οὕτως < λέγει ὁ κύριος > ὁ πλάσας με ἐκ κοιλίας δοῦλον ἑαυτῷ ». καὶ διὰ Ἵερε- «πρὸ τοῦ με πλάσαι σε ἐν κοιλίᾳ ἐπίσταμαί σε, καὶ πρὸ τοῦ σε προελθεῖν ἐκ μήτρας σε». ἐπεὶ οὖν καὶ κατὰ πνεῦμα ἦν γεγεννημένος ὁ λόγος καὶ χατὰ σάρκα, ἅτε δὴ καὶ θεὸς καὶ ἄνθρωπος ὦν, εὐλόγως ὁ προφήτης μήτραν πατρὸς καὶ μητρὸς ὠνόμασεν, ὃ εἰ μὴ οὕτως νοηθείη, γέλοιόν τισι δόξῃ. τῇ γὰρ θηλείᾳ μόνῃ φύσει < μήτρα > παρακολουθεῖ.
laughable. For the term "womb" can only well follow after the female nature alone. Now here he said: "And of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother" so that precisely you might understand that the Word is begotten of two substances, of God and of the Virgin. That is why he adds again saying:
of your mother the prophet proclaims in advance a spiritual mystery. He could, in effect, say: "And of the blessing of the womb of your mother", in order, by this expression, to indicate Mary, by the womb of whom the Word was nine months carried. Well! it is not that which he said, but rather he says: "And of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother (288)." Of these two things, by having thus joined them together, he made of them but one, so that it might be well understood that to this
person belongs <both that which is according to the spirit and that which is> according to the flesh (289). For the Word drew his origin from a heart of a father and from holy [entrails], born <as he was> (290) from a womb of a father (291). As also well, through the prophet, he (the Father) says: "My heart has uttered a good Word." On the other hand, according to the flesh, in the last times, he drew his origin from a virginal womb after having been carried there nine months, so that, after being born a second time, from a womb of a mother also, he might appear visible (292). <As also well, through the prophet, (he himself) says: "Thus says the Lord> (293) who formed me from the womb (of my mother) to be his servant." And, through Jeremiah, (the Father) says: "Before forming you in the womb (of your mother), I knew you; and before you had come out of her womb, I sanctified you." Since then the Word was begotten both according to the spirit and according to the flesh, as being in fact both God and man, it is with

Q> ἤοφοῦφροῦο δὺδ Θωθρροῦο « Ὑπερίσχυσας ἐπ᾽ εὐλογίαις ὀρέων μονίμων ἐπ᾽ ἐπιθυμίαις θινῶν ἀεννάων ». ὑπὲρ γὰρ πάντας ἀρχάς, προφήτας τε καὶ ἀποστόλους, ὑπερίσχυσεν καὶ ἐδοξάσθη, ὑψωθεὶς ὑπὸ τοῦ θεοῦ, τὴν πᾶσαν εὐλογίαν ἐπὶ κεφαλῆς κληρονομῶν. πάντων γὰρ τῶν ἁγίων κεφαλὴ ὁ Χριστὸς ἦν. δυδύφρογῶφυυ J Ὀδ δ)». > ὠτδοῦοςυ- φῦ > ρῶσοῦ > οϑῶς- φγῶονδῃβοῦο m3 Առաւել ղաւրացար լաւրծնուԹիւնս լերանց կայնոց եւ ի յանկութիւնս բլրոց ծշտնջենաւորաց ։ Քան զաճենալն ճայլրապետսն, զմարգարես եւ լառաքեալսն զաւրա- ցաւ, եւ փառաւորեցաւ, բարձրացեալ յաստուծոլ, զամենայն աւրծնութիւն ի վերայ գլխոլ ժառանգեալ τ Քանզի ամենայն սրբոց դլուխ Բրիստոս Է « Ὑπερίσχυσας ἐπ᾽ εὐλογίαις ὀρέων μονίμων καὶ ἐπ᾽ ἐπιθυμίαις θινῶν ἀεννάων ». ὑπὲρ γὰρ πάντας ἀρχάς, προφήτας τε καὶ ἀποστόλους, ὑπερίσχυσεν καὶ ἐδοξάσθη, ὑψωθεὶς ὑπὸ τοῦ θεοῦ, τὴν πᾶσαν εὐλογίαν ἐπὶ κεφαλῆς κληρονομῶν. πάντων γὰρ τῶν ἁγίων κεφαλὴ ὁ Χριστὸς ἦν.
"You have prevailed over the blessings of the mountains that endure (295), and over the desires (296) of the eternal hills." For over all principalities (297), prophets and apostles, he (the Word) prevailed, and was glorified, exalted as he is by God inheriting; upon his head the blessing entire. Of all the Saints, in effect, Christ was head.

"They will be, in effect, (these blessings), says (Jacob), upon the head of Joseph and upon the top of the skull of the brothers whom he commanded." "Of his brothers", he said: not his brothers according to the flesh, for those he rejected, but those who were esteemed (298) to be his brothers according to the spirit, those to whom the Lord said: "My brothers and co-heirs." It is upon those that Jacob said that the blessings would come, those whom the Savior himself could regard as his own brothers (299).

### Benjamin

"Benjamin! ravenous wolf! In the morning, he will devour again; and in the evening, he gives (299 bis) the food." "Ravenous wolf": it is therefore very obviously the Apostle Paul that he has, by this name, designated; (Paul) who was born of the tribe of Benjamin; ravenous wolf at the beginning (300), tearing and devouring the sheep of the Church. As also well Paul himself confesses, saying: "I have nothing of what is needed to be called an apostle, because, to excess, I persecuted the Church of God. But it is by the grace of God that I am what I am."

ուտեր ղոչխարս եկեղեցւոլ, որպես ինքն իսկ Պաւղոս խոստովանի, ասելով` ե չեճ արժանի կոչել առաքեալ, վասն վի առաւել Հալածեցի զեկ « եղեցին աստուծոլ, ալլ > շնորգաւք աստուծո եմ ո եմ ։ Διὰ τοῦτο δὲ καὶ Ραχὴλ դերկնաւոր կերակուրն, նորա յառաջ լաղգս լԲրիստոս քարոզեալ, որով Հաւատայեալք եւ ճեք՝ տացուք աւրգնութիւն աստուծոլ τ Նճա փառք լաւիտեանս ε
It is moreover for this that Rachel, she too, giving birth to Benjamin, called him then by this name "Son of my pain," prophesying what was to happen, namely that Paul, born of the tribe of Benjamin, was to cause pain (301) and tribulation to his own mother, that is to say to the Church, by causing to perish all those who invoked the name of the Lord. (Paul) who, after having been, at the beginning, a blasphemer and persecutor, later, once converted, distributed to all the spiritual and celestial food, being himself the first to announce to the Gentiles Christ (302); to whom keeping our faith (303), let us give, we too, praise to God (304), for to Him is the glory in the ages of ages. Amen.

[p. 116] {#po.27.p116}
